> AGENT

VibeComfy is an **instrument**, not a conductor.

It gives an agent a concrete capability: inspect, edit, build, and run ComfyUI workflows through CLI/tooling. The **conductor** would be the higher-level agent or orchestration layer that decides what to do, sequences steps, and calls VibeComfy when ComfyUI workflow work is needed.

So in Arnold terms: **VibeComfy = tool/instrument for ComfyUI workflow manipulation**.

Sources I checked: [VibeComfy GitHub-linked description](https://sd114.wiki/23077.html), [Playbooks comfy-edit skill](https://playbooks.com/skills/peteromallet/vibecomfy/comfy-edit).

> DEVELOPER

should be housed inside ArtAgents?

> AGENT

I’ll use the ArtAgents context briefly since this is an architecture placement question, then I’ll answer at the boundary level rather than overfitting to the current repo.

> AGENT

Yes, but only as a **performer/instrument integration**, not as ArtAgents core.

I’d house a thin ArtAgents wrapper around VibeComfy inside ArtAgents, probably under something like:

`artagents/performers/comfy.py`

That performer would call VibeComfy to do concrete ComfyUI work: build/edit/submit workflows, generate images/video assets, inspect workflow outputs, and return artifacts into the ArtAgents file-based pipeline.

I would **not** bury VibeComfy itself inside ArtAgents unless the two repos are meant to merge permanently. VibeComfy has independent value as a general ComfyUI agent interface. ArtAgents should depend on it, call it, and standardize its outputs for Reigh/ArtAgents pipelines.

So the clean split is:

- **VibeComfy**: standalone instrument for ComfyUI workflows.
- **ArtAgents performer**: adapter that lets conductors invoke VibeComfy.
- **ArtAgents conductors**: decide when ComfyUI generation belongs in a larger edit/generative pipeline.

That preserves the architecture: conductors coordinate; performers execute; VibeComfy remains reusable infrastructure.

> AGENT

For ArtAgents, the “custom elements” are two different things:

1. **Agent/workflow elements**
   These are already in ArtAgents:
   [artagents/performers/curated](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/performers/curated) and [artagents/conductors/curated](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/conductors/curated).

   VibeComfy is already modeled correctly as a curated performer:
   [artagents/performers/curated/vibecomfy](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/performers/curated/vibecomfy).

2. **Visual/custom render primitives**
   These are supposed to live at workspace root, outside the ArtAgents repo folder:
   `../effects/<id>/`
   `../animations/<id>/`
   `../transitions/<id>/`
   `../themes/<theme-id>/effects/<id>/`
   `../themes/<theme-id>/animations/<id>/`
   `../themes/<theme-id>/transitions/<id>/`

   The code expects that via [artagents/effects_catalog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/effects_catalog.py:34) and [remotion/webpack-alias.mjs](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/remotion/webpack-alias.mjs:5).

Default custom elements should be treated as **stdlib / baseline primitives**, not user content. They should be versioned, read-only by default, namespaced clearly, and overridable only through an explicit theme-level element with a warning. The current code already has the right override behavior: theme primitives win over workspace primitives and emit a warning.

Are they well organized right now? **Partly, but not fully.** The architecture is sensible: performers/conductors are clean, and render primitives have a good folder contract. But the current workspace looks under-populated and a bit confusing: `../effects`, `../animations`, `../transitions`, and `../themes` do not exist in this checkout; generated registries are empty; and the only concrete primitive examples I found are test fixtures under [tests/fixtures/themes](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/tests/fixtures/themes). That means the contract is organized, but the actual default primitive catalog is not yet clearly materialized.

> DEVELOPER

What would you suggest? How should we populate teh defaul custom nodes? Git pull'd via a db?

> AGENT

I’d split this into **catalog metadata in DB**, **actual code in git/packages**.

Do **not** store default custom node/component code directly in a DB. Use the DB as an index/control plane, not as the source of truth for executable code.

Suggested model:

**1. Default elements live in a git-backed package**
Create a canonical repo/package for baseline primitives, for example:

`banodoco-default-elements`
or
`@banodoco/default-artagents-primitives`

Inside it:

```text
effects/
  text-card/
  art-card/
  section-hook/
animations/
  fade/
  fade-up/
  type-on/
transitions/
  cross-fade/
themes/
  banodoco-default/
    theme.json
    effects/
    animations/
    transitions/
manifest.json
```

Each element keeps the existing contract:

```text
component.tsx
schema.json
defaults.json
meta.json
```

**2. ArtAgents installs/syncs them into the workspace**
ArtAgents should treat defaults like an installed stdlib dependency. Possible install target:

```text
.artagents/elements/default/
```

or, better if you want the current registry code to work with minimal changes:

```text
../effects/
../animations/
../transitions/
../themes/banodoco-default/
```

But I’d avoid writing directly into root workspace folders long term. Cleaner would be to teach `effects_catalog.py` and `gen_effect_registry.py` to scan multiple roots:

```text
.artagents/elements/default/effects
.artagents/elements/installed/<package>/effects
../effects
../themes
```

Priority order:

1. active theme
2. user workspace primitives
3. installed/default primitives

That gives users overrides without mutating the defaults.

**3. DB stores catalog + version pinning**
The DB should store records like:

```json
{
  "id": "text-card",
  "kind": "effect",
  "package": "banodoco/default-elements",
  "version": "1.2.0",
  "git_url": "https://github.com/banodoco/default-elements.git",
  "git_ref": "v1.2.0",
  "default": true,
  "mandatory": false,
  "description": "Basic text card primitive"
}
```

Then ArtAgents can do:

```bash
python3 pipeline.py elements sync --defaults
python3 pipeline.py elements list
python3 pipeline.py elements inspect text-card
```

The DB answers “what should exist?” Git answers “what is the code?”

**4. Default custom nodes should be curated, not user-editable**
Treat default nodes/elements as:

- **Pinned**: installed at a known version/ref.
- **Reproducible**: renders should say which primitive package/version was active.
- **Read-only**: user changes go into workspace/theme overrides, not inside the default package.
- **Small and boring**: defaults should be robust primitives, not brand-specific experiments.
- **Namespaced**: avoid collisions with user/theme primitives.

**5. VibeComfy-specific custom nodes**
If you mean **ComfyUI custom nodes**, I’d use the same pattern:

- DB catalog says which Comfy custom node repos are approved/default.
- Git/pip install supplies the actual nodes.
- VibeComfy/ArtAgents performer installs them into an isolated Comfy environment.
- Workflows reference required node packages and versions.

So: **yes to DB-driven discovery and policy; no to DB-stored executable code**. Pull code from git/package registries, pin versions, cache locally, and let themes/workspace override defaults explicitly.

> DEVELOPER

Everything shouod be use-editible, should every piece of function be a custom node?

> AGENT

Everything can be **user-editable** without making every piece of function a custom node.

I’d avoid “everything is a custom node.” That usually becomes too granular, hard to version, hard to test, and awkward for agents to reason about. Instead:

**Make user-editable things into nodes when they are product-level units:**

- A render primitive: `text-card`, `art-card`, `cta-card`
- A workflow performer: `vibecomfy.run`, `youtube.upload`, `thumbnail.generate`
- A conductor recipe: `hype-cut`, `event-talks`, `thumbnail-maker`
- A Comfy workflow template: `portrait-upscale`, `product-shot`, `style-transfer`
- A reusable transformation with clear inputs/outputs: `crop-review`, `audio-understand`, `sprite-sheet`

**Do not make tiny implementation functions into nodes:**

- JSON parsing helpers
- path normalization
- ffmpeg argument formatting
- schema validation internals
- Remotion timing math helpers
- low-level Python/TS utility functions

Those should stay normal code. They can still be editable through source/plugins, but they should not appear as first-class user “nodes.”

The useful split is:

```text
Core runtime
  Stable boring code. Not usually edited by users.

Default editable nodes/elements
  Shipped by Banodoco/ArtAgents, visible to users, forkable/overrideable.

User workspace nodes/elements
  Created or modified by users.

Theme/project overrides
  Brand-specific or project-specific variants.
```

For “default custom nodes,” I’d treat them as **editable templates, not sacred internals**:

1. Defaults install read-only into a managed location.
2. When a user edits one, ArtAgents copies it into their workspace/theme as an override.
3. The override shadows the default.
4. The original default remains available for reset/diff/upgrade.

So the UX is “everything is editable,” but the architecture is “defaults are forked before mutation.”

A good rule of thumb:

> If a user would name it, configure it, reuse it, swap it, publish it, or ask an agent to improve it, it should probably be a node/element. If it only exists to help another node work, keep it as internal code.

For VibeComfy/Comfy specifically: every **workflow template**, **custom Comfy node package**, and **ArtAgents performer around Comfy** can be node-like. But every Python function inside those packages should not become an ArtAgents custom node.

> DEVELOPER

So VibeComfy remains an executor or instrument?

> AGENT

Yes: **VibeComfy remains an instrument/executor**.

More precisely:

- **Instrument** in the architecture: it gives agents concrete ComfyUI capabilities.
- **Executor** at runtime: it runs/validates/edits/submits Comfy workflows.
- **Not a conductor**: it should not own high-level creative sequencing across ArtAgents/Reigh.

ArtAgents should call it through a performer like `external.vibecomfy.run` or `external.vibecomfy.validate`. A conductor may decide: “make this asset with Comfy,” then invoke the VibeComfy performer to execute that step.

> DEVELOPER

So what do we need to change?

> AGENT

We need to change naming, discovery, and default element handling more than the core conductor/performer split.

**Recommended Changes**
1. Keep VibeComfy as a performer/instrument.
   Current location is right:
   [artagents/performers/curated/vibecomfy](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/performers/curated/vibecomfy)

   I’d just make the metadata clearer: “executor/instrument for Comfy workflows,” not a conductor.

2. Add an explicit **elements/nodes layer** for user-editable creative units.
   Today render primitives are implicitly expected at workspace root:

   ```text
   ../effects/
   ../animations/
   ../transitions/
   ../themes/
   ```

   That contract exists, but the default catalog is not actually present. I’d add a first-class ArtAgents command surface:

   ```bash
   python3 pipeline.py elements list
   python3 pipeline.py elements inspect text-card
   python3 pipeline.py elements fork text-card --theme my-brand
   python3 pipeline.py elements sync --defaults
   ```

3. Populate default custom elements from a git/package source, not DB code.
   DB should track catalog/version/policy. Git/package should contain executable code.

   Defaults should install into a managed location such as:

   ```text
   .artagents/elements/default/
     effects/
     animations/
     transitions/
     themes/
   ```

4. Update primitive discovery to scan multiple roots.
   Change [artagents/effects_catalog.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/effects_catalog.py) and [scripts/gen_effect_registry.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/scripts/gen_effect_registry.py) so priority is:

   ```text
   active theme overrides
   user workspace primitives
   installed custom packages
   default primitives
   ```

   This preserves “everything user-editable” without mutating shipped defaults.

5. Add fork-on-edit behavior.
   When a user edits a default node/element, copy it into:

   ```text
   ../themes/<theme-id>/effects/<id>/
   ```

   or:

   ```text
   ../effects/<id>/
   ```

   Then the override shadows the default. This gives reset/diff/upgrade behavior.

6. Clarify vocabulary.
   I’d use:
   - **Conductors**: orchestrate.
   - **Performers/instruments**: execute tools like VibeComfy.
   - **Elements/nodes/primitives**: user-editable creative/render/workflow units.

The main missing piece is a real default element catalog plus install/fork/discovery mechanics. VibeComfy itself does not need to become a conductor or be deeply embedded.

> DEVELOPER

And do we have a way to install/update requremetns based on a new ly cused custom node?

> AGENT

I’ll check the current installer paths for performers and primitives to see whether dependency installation exists for user-created nodes or only for performer packages.

> AGENT

Partially.

**What exists now:** yes for **performers**. External performers can declare dependencies, and ArtAgents can install/update them into deterministic venvs:

```bash
python3 pipeline.py performers install external.vibecomfy.run
```

That uses [artagents/performers/install.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/artagents/performers/install.py:80). It supports:

- folder-level `requirements.txt`
- folder-level `pyproject.toml`
- inline `isolation.requirements`
- shared package envs via `PACKAGE_ID`, which VibeComfy already uses

So newly created **performer nodes** have a workable dependency story.

**What does not exist yet:** no equivalent installer for user-created **render/custom elements** like:

```text
effects/<id>/
animations/<id>/
transitions/<id>/
themes/<theme>/effects/<id>/
```

Those are discovered and codegen’d, but there is no `elements install` / `elements sync` flow that reads per-element requirements and updates Python/npm dependencies.

I’d add that.

Proposed contract for a custom element:

```text
effects/my-card/
  component.tsx
  schema.json
  defaults.json
  meta.json
  package.json        # optional JS deps
  requirements.txt    # optional Python deps, only if generation/tooling needs Python
```

Then add:

```bash
python3 pipeline.py elements install my-card
python3 pipeline.py elements install --all
python3 pipeline.py elements sync --defaults
python3 pipeline.py elements update my-card
```

For render primitives, most dependencies are JS-side, so install should update an isolated or managed element dependency area, not randomly mutate `remotion/package.json` without intent. For Comfy custom nodes, the installer should install into the VibeComfy/Comfy environment, not ArtAgents’ main Python env.

So the honest answer: **we have this for performer/instrument nodes, not yet for creative/render custom elements or Comfy custom-node packages.** That should be one of the next changes.

> AGENT

Yes. The current structure is workable, but there are confusing parts that will get worse once user-editable nodes/elements become first-class.

Main issues:

1. **Vocabulary is inconsistent**
   README says **players** and **nodes**, but code says **performers** and **conductors**. Render primitives are a third concept but are not named clearly in the top-level map.

   I’d standardize:
   - `conductors`: orchestration
   - `performers`: executable instruments
   - `elements` or `primitives`: user-editable creative/render units
   - avoid “players” unless you rename performers everywhere

2. **Default custom elements are implied, not present**
   Code expects workspace-level folders:

   ```text
   ../effects/
   ../animations/
   ../transitions/
   ../themes/
   ```

   But they do not exist in this checkout, and generated registries are empty. That makes the system look half-wired: the contract exists, but the default catalog is not materialized.

3. **Render primitive ownership is split confusingly**
   ArtAgents owns the generator at:

   [scripts/gen_effect_registry.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/scripts/gen_effect_registry.py)

   But it writes generated files into:

   ```text
   ../packages/timeline-composition/typescript/src/
   ```

   while `remotion/src/*.generated.ts` are only shims. That may be intentional, but it is not obvious from the repo map. A contributor will wonder whether primitives belong in `ArtAgents/remotion`, workspace root, or `packages/timeline-composition`.

4. **No first-class `elements` domain**
   Performers and conductors have registries, CLI, folder discovery, validation, install flow, catalog loading. Elements do not. They are just conventions around folders plus codegen.

   That is the biggest structural gap if “everything user-editable” is the goal.

5. **Dependency install exists only for performers**
   `pipeline.py performers install ...` is solid. There is no equivalent for custom render elements or Comfy custom-node packages. So a newly created element can compile only if its deps happen to already exist.

6. **Built-in vs curated is slightly muddy**
   Built-in conductors physically live under `artagents/conductors/curated/...`, then `load_builtin_conductors()` promotes some to built-in. That works, but the folder name says curated while the registry treats some as built-in. Same general risk exists around “curated external” performers.

My suggested cleanup target:

```text
artagents/
  conductors/
  performers/
  elements/          # registry, install, sync, fork, inspect
    schema.py
    registry.py
    install.py
    cli.py

.artagents/
  elements/
    defaults/
    installed/

../effects/          # user workspace overrides
../animations/
../transitions/
../themes/
```

And update docs to say: ArtAgents has **conductors, performers, and elements**. VibeComfy remains a performer. Default custom nodes/elements become installed editable templates, forked into workspace/theme on edit.

What's the difference between performeners and elements? Default sjhould be bundled in the repo? And they should install by default. We should hav ea clear entry point combined with the text. VibneCOmfy and others hould appear by default - but we should figure out how to include ALL teh nodes that they have in their skill doc and maybe promkpt

> AGENT

**Performers vs Elements**
**Performers** are executable capabilities. They do work.

Examples:
- `external.vibecomfy.run`
- `external.vibecomfy.validate`
- `builtin.render`
- `upload.youtube`
- future `comfy.install_nodes`
- future `image.upscale`

A performer has inputs, outputs, command/runtime, install requirements, and can be called by a conductor.

**Elements** are user-editable creative/building blocks. They are things a performer or renderer uses.

Examples:
- Remotion effect: `text-card`
- animation: `fade-up`
- transition: `cross-fade`
- Comfy workflow template: `portrait-upscale`
- Comfy custom-node package declaration: `comfyui-impact-pack`
- prompt template / style preset

An element should be forkable/editable. A performer executes or materializes it.

So: **VibeComfy is a performer/instrument. The workflows, Comfy node packs, prompt templates, and render primitives it knows about are elements/nodes.**

**Defaults**
Yes, defaults should be **bundled in the repo initially** and installed by default.

That gives a working out-of-box repo:

```bash
git clone ...
python3 -m pip install -r requirements.txt
cd remotion && npm install
python3 pipeline.py elements list
python3 pipeline.py performers list
```

Later, bundled defaults can become a git/package catalog. But first we need a reliable baseline.

I’d structure it like:

```text
artagents/defaults/
  elements/
    effects/
    animations/
    transitions/
    comfy/
      workflows/
      node_packs/
      prompts/
```

On setup/sync, they install/copy into managed state:

```text
.artagents/elements/default/
```

User edits fork into:

```text
../effects/
../animations/
../transitions/
../themes/<theme>/
```

or for Comfy:

```text
.artagents/elements/user/comfy/
```

**Install By Default**
Yes. Default elements and default performers should be available without extra flags.

For dependencies:
- bundled JS render elements should be covered by `remotion/package.json`
- bundled Python performers use repo requirements or performer venvs
- VibeComfy should be visible by default, but running it can either auto-install its venv or show one exact install command

I’d prefer:

```bash
python3 pipeline.py setup
```

does:
- install default elements
- install/sync default performer environments where safe
- regenerate registries
- verify VibeComfy availability
- print what is ready vs missing

**Clear Entry Point**
Add a combined agent/user entrypoint:

```text
AGENTS.md
```

This should say:

1. Start here.
2. Run `git status --short`.
3. Run `python3 pipeline.py doctor`.
4. Understand the model:
   - conductors orchestrate
   - performers execute
   - elements are editable building blocks
5. Use:
   - `pipeline.py conductors list`
   - `pipeline.py performers list`
   - `pipeline.py elements list`
   - `pipeline.py setup`
6. Generated files go under `runs/`.
7. Do not edit defaults directly; fork them.

Then `README.md` can stay human-facing, and `SKILL.md` can stay detailed agent operating rules.

**VibeComfy And “All Nodes”**
For VibeComfy, we should not just expose two performers. We should ingest its skill/doc/catalog into ArtAgents as element metadata.

Something like:

```text
artagents/defaults/elements/vibecomfy/
  manifest.json
  SKILL.md
  performers.json
  workflows/
  node_packs/
  prompts/
```

Then ArtAgents can show:

```bash
python3 pipeline.py performers list
python3 pipeline.py elements list --source vibecomfy
python3 pipeline.py elements inspect vibecomfy.workflow.portrait-upscale
python3 pipeline.py elements install --source vibecomfy
```

The VibeComfy `SKILL.md` should become agent-facing context, but the actual node list should be structured metadata, not scraped free text every time. The prompt can summarize the available nodes/elements for agents.

So the implementation target should be:

- bundled defaults
- default install/sync
- `AGENTS.md`
- `pipeline.py setup`
- `pipeline.py doctor`
- `pipeline.py elements ...`
- VibeComfy visible by default as performer/instrument
- VibeComfy’s workflows/node packs/prompts represented as elements
- docs explain exactly what to do first

> AGENT

Assuming “doctors” means **conductors**: yes, the megaplan should be framed around three public concepts:

```text
conductors  -> decide / orchestrate
performers  -> execute capabilities
elements    -> user-editable building blocks used by performers/renderers
```

I would explicitly **not** enforce the internal structure of a performer. VibeComfy can expose ArtAgents metadata, but its internal Comfy workflow/node organization should remain VibeComfy-owned. ArtAgents should only require a manifest/API boundary.

**Megaplan Shape**

**Goal:** make ArtAgents self-explanatory and working out of the box with default performers/elements, clear onboarding, and flexible performer-owned internals.

**Success criteria:**
- `README.md` clearly explains what an agent/user should do first.
- `AGENTS.md` or equivalent entrypoint exists and points to README/SKILL without duplicating too much.
- `pipeline.py doctor` checks setup health.
- `pipeline.py setup` installs/syncs bundled defaults.
- `pipeline.py conductors list`, `performers list`, and `elements list` all work.
- VibeComfy appears by default as an available performer/instrument.
- VibeComfy-associated workflows/prompts/node packs can be represented as elements, but ArtAgents does not dictate VibeComfy’s internal repo layout.
- Default elements are bundled in-repo initially and installed by default.
- User edits fork defaults instead of mutating canonical defaults.
- Tests cover registry, install/sync, fork, docs command visibility, and VibeComfy default registration.

**Plan Steps**

1. **Onboarding and Vocabulary Audit**
   Review `README.md`, `SKILL.md`, `pipeline.py`, conductor/performer docs, and current VibeComfy performer metadata.
   Decide final public language:
   - conductors
   - performers/instruments
   - elements/primitives
   - doctor/setup

2. **Entry Point**
   Add `AGENTS.md` as the short “start here” file:
   - read README
   - run `git status --short`
   - run `python3 pipeline.py doctor`
   - list conductors/performers/elements
   - use `runs/` for outputs
   - defaults are forked before edit

   Update README’s existing agent prompt to reference this flow.

3. **Elements Domain**
   Add `artagents/elements/` with:
   - schema
   - registry
   - discovery
   - install/sync helpers
   - fork helper
   - CLI

   Commands:
   ```bash
   python3 pipeline.py elements list
   python3 pipeline.py elements inspect <id>
   python3 pipeline.py elements sync --defaults
   python3 pipeline.py elements install <id|--all>
   python3 pipeline.py elements fork <id> --theme <theme>
   ```

4. **Bundled Defaults**
   Add repo-bundled defaults under something like:

   ```text
   artagents/defaults/elements/
     effects/
     animations/
     transitions/
     vibecomfy/
       manifest.json
       workflows/
       prompts/
       node_packs/
   ```

   Then sync into managed state:

   ```text
   .artagents/elements/default/
   ```

5. **Discovery Priority**
   Update render primitive discovery/codegen to scan:

   ```text
   active theme
   user workspace overrides
   installed/custom packages
   bundled/default managed elements
   ```

   Theme/user override wins. Default remains resettable.

6. **Setup and Doctor**
   Add:
   ```bash
   python3 pipeline.py setup
   python3 pipeline.py doctor
   ```

   `setup` syncs bundled defaults and regenerates registries.
   `doctor` checks:
   - Python deps
   - Remotion deps
   - default elements synced
   - generated registries current enough
   - VibeComfy performer visible
   - external performer env status

7. **VibeComfy Integration**
   Keep current performer location:
   `artagents/performers/curated/vibecomfy`

   Improve metadata/docs so VibeComfy is described as an executor/instrument.
   Add VibeComfy element metadata, but keep internals flexible. ArtAgents should understand only:
   - element id
   - kind: workflow / prompt / node_pack / template
   - source: vibecomfy
   - install/runtime hints
   - optional skill/doc pointer

8. **Dependency Handling**
   Keep performer dependency install as-is.
   Add element install support:
   - JS deps from element/package metadata
   - Python requirements only when declared
   - Comfy/VibeComfy deps delegated to the VibeComfy performer environment

   Do not mutate global envs silently.

9. **Tests**
   Add focused tests for:
   - bundled default discovery
   - element list/inspect
   - fork-on-edit path
   - setup/doctor CLI paths
   - discovery priority
   - VibeComfy appears by default
   - performer internals are not schema-enforced

10. **Docs Pass**
   Update README/SKILL/AGENTS with the final mental model and exact commands.

This is a **standard robustness code megaplan**, not just a doc plan. It crosses docs, CLI, registries, install behavior, render discovery, and tests.

> DEVELOPER

What needs to be done in the clean=up?

> AGENT

Cleanup needed falls into three buckets.

**Must Fix Before Merge**
1. **Review the giant diff**
   The change touches a lot of files. We need to inspect for accidental churn, especially generated Remotion files, docs, wrappers, and the pre-existing dirty skill files.

2. **Bootstrap-safe `doctor`**
   `python3 pipeline.py doctor` can fail in a lean env before it diagnoses missing deps, because `pipeline.py` imports modules that require `jsonschema`. Root dispatch should route `doctor`/`setup` before heavy imports, or lazy-load those dependencies.

3. **Clarify `setup`**
   Current `setup` is more like element/default setup. Either expand it to report Python/Node setup steps, or rename/docs it as element setup.

4. **Generated files decision**
   Decide whether new generated Remotion `.js`, `.d.ts`, `.map` files are intended committed artifacts. If yes, keep them all. If no, ignore/regenerate consistently.

5. **Run final validation locally**
   Megaplan reports full `pytest` passing and `tests/test_effects_catalog.py` passed directly. We should rerun focused tests plus `pytest` once after cleanup. Remotion smoke is still blocked by `No available ports found`.

**Should Fix Soon**
6. **Canonical naming leaks**
   API/JSON still leaks legacy names like `child_performers`, `child_conductors`, `Performer*`, `Conductor*`, `.artagents/performers`, and `primitive`. We should either migrate public output to `child_executors` / `child_orchestrators`, or explicitly document those as compatibility internals.

7. **Remaining folder weirdness**
   `orchestrators` / `executors` exist, but some canonical packages are still facades over `conductors` / `performers`. Also:
   - built-in orchestrators still live under `conductors/curated`
   - VibeComfy/Moirae still live under `performers/curated`
   - `artagents/executors/builtin.py` and `artagents/executors/builtin/` both exist; reviewer says the file is likely shadowed and should be removed/folded

8. **Element dependency resolution**
   Element JS deps install under `.artagents/elements/.../node`, but Webpack currently resolves `remotion/node_modules`, not those element-local `node_modules`.

9. **Element ID validation**
   Element IDs can apparently allow values that generate invalid TypeScript component names. Tighten validation to match codegen expectations.

10. **Custom element authoring**
   Add `pipeline.py elements new effects my-id` or docs for creating a new element from scratch. Forking is clear; greenfield creation is not.

**Docs Polish**
11. **Reduce doc duplication**
   README, AGENTS, SKILL, and architecture docs repeat a lot. Make `docs/architecture.md` the canonical model and let the others point to it.

12. **Fix stale wording**
   Reviewers noted stale lines like “executors have begun moving” after the move completed, and `effects_catalog.py` described as “to be moved” though it is now a facade.

13. **Make mutating commands obvious**
   Mark commands like `elements fork`, `setup --apply`, `elements install --apply` as writing to local state.

I’d do cleanup as a smaller follow-up pass, starting with `doctor` bootstrap safety, generated-file policy, and diff review.

> AGENT

If an executor requires a completely different skill, it should still live inside that executor folder.

Default shape:

```text
artagents/executors/vibecomfy/
  executor.py
  metadata.json
  SKILL.md
  requirements.txt
```

If it needs more than a snippet:

```text
artagents/executors/vibecomfy/
  executor.py
  metadata.json
  SKILL.md
  requirements.txt
  skills/
    comfy-workflow-authoring/
      SKILL.md
      examples/
      references/
```

So the rule should be:

- `SKILL.md` at executor root is the entry point.
- If the executor needs deeper agent behavior, that root `SKILL.md` points to local sub-skills.
- Those sub-skills remain owned by the executor, not a global folder.
- The global executor registry only indexes: id, summary, command, deps, and skill entrypoint path.

That keeps the simple case simple, but still supports something like VibeComfy having rich, specialized internal agent instructions.

> AGENT

Yes. More elegant is to separate **executor identity** from **agent guidance**, but colocate both.

I’d use this shape:

```text
artagents/executors/vibecomfy/
  executor.yaml
  SKILL.md
  src/
  requirements.txt
  assets/
```

Where:

`executor.yaml` is machine-readable:
```yaml
id: vibecomfy
name: VibeComfy
summary: Runs ComfyUI workflows for image/video generation.
entrypoint: src/run.py
skill: SKILL.md
requirements:
  python: requirements.txt
```

`SKILL.md` is the human/agent-readable entry point.

If the executor needs a much richer skill, don’t create a separate global skill system. Let `SKILL.md` become a tiny router:

```md
# VibeComfy

Use this executor for ComfyUI workflow execution.

For workflow editing, read: ./guides/workflow-authoring.md
For node requirements, read: ./guides/nodes.md
For prompt conventions, read: ./guides/prompting.md
```

Then:

```text
artagents/executors/vibecomfy/
  executor.yaml
  SKILL.md
  guides/
    workflow-authoring.md
    nodes.md
    prompting.md
  src/
  workflows/
  requirements.txt
```

So the elegant rule is:

**Every executor has exactly one obvious agent entry point: `SKILL.md`.**

That file can be tiny or rich. If the executor needs more depth, it links to local docs/guides inside the executor folder. No nested “skills inside skills” unless we discover a real need. This keeps onboarding dead simple: agents inspect `executor.yaml`, then read `SKILL.md`.

> DEVELOPER

Shoudl this be a light megaplan?

> AGENT

Yes. This should be a **light megaplan**, not hand-rolled in one pass.

Reason: the core decision is clear now, but the migration touches a lot of surfaces:

- executor folder shape
- orchestrator folder shape
- registry/schema/API naming
- CLI commands
- docs/onboarding
- tests
- old `performers` / `conductors` shims
- top-level executor files in `artagents/`
- VibeComfy/Moirae skill placement

A light megaplan is the right size because we are not debating the architecture anymore. The architecture is:

```text
artagents/executors/<executor_id>/
  executor.yaml
  SKILL.md
  run.py or src/

artagents/orchestrators/<orchestrator_id>/
  orchestrator.yaml
  SKILL.md
  run.py or src/
```

No `performers` public concept. No `conductors` public concept. No global skill pile. One runnable thing equals one self-contained folder.

I did start direct migration by replacing some executor facade files with executor-native copies, but I should stop there and let a light megaplan own the rest so it can sequence the cleanup cleanly and verify after each stage.

> AGENT

The planner surfaced four design questions; I’m resolving them now rather than handing them back. The decisions are: no legacy command dispatch, JSON-compatible YAML to avoid a new parser dependency, simple folder slugs like `render` with stable IDs inside manifests, and old `performer`/`conductor` env/catalog names removed from the public surface.

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/ArtAgents/.megaplan/plans/folderize-executors-orchestrators/plan_v1.md\n@@\n The target is a clean public model with only orchestrators, executors, and elements. That means moving metadata and runtime ownership into folderized executor/orchestrator directories, deleting public alias dispatch and packages, updating docs/tests, and preserving only root/bin entrypoints needed to launch commands.\n+\n+Resolved decisions:\n+- Legacy command tokens (`performers`, `instruments`, `conductors`, `primitives`) should not receive special compatibility dispatch. They may fail as ordinary unknown/invalid commands, but should not be advertised or routed.\n+- `executor.yaml` and `orchestrator.yaml` should use a stdlib-friendly JSON-compatible YAML subset unless the repo already has a YAML dependency. Do not add a parser dependency just for metadata.\n+- Folder names should be simple filesystem slugs, while manifest IDs remain stable. For example, `artagents/executors/render/executor.yaml` may declare `id: builtin.render`, and `artagents/executors/vibecomfy/executor.yaml` may declare both `external.vibecomfy.run` and `external.vibecomfy.validate` if that executor owns multiple commands.\n+- External catalog/env names containing `performer` or `conductor` are old public surface and should be renamed or removed from current docs/CLI. Keep only private migration handling if an in-repo caller still needs it during the pass.\n@@\n-1. Support `artagents/executors/<executor_id>/executor.yaml` as the primary metadata source.\n+1. > AGENT

The correct rule is: if a module has a user-facing `main()` or CLI parser, it should either be an executor, an orchestrator, or a root setup/doctor/system command. `sprite_sheet.py`, `boundary_candidates.py`, `inspect_cut.py`, `human_notes.py`, `open_in_reigh.py`, `asset_cache.py`, `publish.py`, `publish_youtube.py`, `reigh_data.py`, and `generate_image.py` all qualify for classification; `event_talks.py` and `thumbnail_maker.py` are orchestrator runtimes.

> DEVELOPER

But why are there still some many other executors?

> AGENT

Because the cleanup rule we applied was: **anything that is independently runnable and does concrete work becomes an executor**.

So the count is high for three reasons:

1. The hype pipeline is made of many leaf steps:
   `triage`, `transcribe`, `scenes`, `quote_scout`, `quality_zones`, `shots`, `arrange`, `refine`, `cut`, `render`, `validate`, etc.

2. There are standalone tools that are not pipeline stages:
   `sprite_sheet`, `generate_image`, `boundary_candidates`, `inspect_cut`, `open_in_reigh`, `asset_cache`, `publish`, `reigh_data`.

3. External/default tools also belong there:
   `vibecomfy`, `moirae`, `upload.youtube`.

The deeper issue is not “too many executors” by itself. It is whether all of them should be **agent-facing default executors**.

My recommendation:

- Keep them all physically under `artagents/executors/<executor>/`.
- Add a simple visibility/type distinction in `executor.yaml`, for example:
  - `role: public_tool`
  - `role: pipeline_step`
  - `role: support_tool`
  - `role: external_tool`
- Make `executors list` default to the useful public view.
- Add `executors list --all` for every internal pipeline step.
- Keep orchestrators as the human/agent entrypoints for workflows like `hype`, `understand`, `thumbnail_maker`.

So `sprite_sheet` should be prominent. `vibecomfy` should be prominent. But `pool_merge` or `quality_zones` probably should exist as executors while being presented as “pipeline step executors”, not top-level things a new agent is pushed toward first.

> DEVELOPER

Why do we need a difference? Why are they not just all the same?

> AGENT

They can all be the same **technically**. The difference is mostly for **agent/user UX**, not runtime architecture.

An executor should mean one thing:

> a callable unit that does work, has inputs/outputs, can be inspected, installed, validated, and run.

Under that definition, `sprite_sheet`, `vibecomfy`, `quality_zones`, `render`, `publish`, and `boundary_candidates` are all executors.

The only reason to add labels like `pipeline_step` or `public_tool` is presentation:

- A new agent should not see 31 things and wonder where to start.
- An orchestrator needs to know which executors are internal steps.
- Humans may want a short “useful tools” list and a full “everything” list.

But I would **not** make separate concepts. No separate registry. No separate folder. No different schema.

Best version:

```yaml
id: builtin.sprite_sheet
kind: executor
display:
  surface: default
  group: media_generation
```

```yaml
id: builtin.quality_zones
kind: executor
display:
  surface: advanced
  group: hype_pipeline
```

Same executor model. Same folder shape. Same install/update behavior. Just optional display metadata.

So the answer is: they **are** all the same. We only need lightweight metadata to avoid making onboarding messy.

> DEVELOPER

you're overcomplicating this, they just shouldn't be in two placeds and shoulld all be in the one format, no excedptions

> DEVELOPER

We shoudl figure out better ways to accommodate any complexities

> AGENT

Agreed. The better rule is simpler:

**Every executor lives in exactly one place and one format:**

```text
artagents/executors/<executor_slug>/
  executor.yaml
  SKILL.md
  run.py
  src/        # optional, for complex implementations
  requirements.txt  # optional
  assets/     # optional
  guides/     # optional
```

No top-level runnable executor files. No duplicate `bin` implementation. No “internal executor” exception. No shims.

For complexity, we accommodate it inside the executor folder, not by creating another category:

```text
artagents/executors/sprite_sheet/
  executor.yaml
  SKILL.md
  run.py
  src/
    generation.py
    slicing.py
    preview.py
  assets/
  requirements.txt
```

Same for `vibecomfy`, `render`, `quality_zones`, `generate_image`, etc. If it is simple, `run.py` can contain most of it. If it is complex, `run.py` is a thin entrypoint into local `src/`.

For orchestrators, same principle:

```text
artagents/orchestrators/<orchestrator_slug>/
  orchestrator.yaml
  SKILL.md
  run.py
  src/        # optional
```

The cleanup target should be:

- Move executor implementation code out of top-level `artagents/*.py` into `artagents/executors/<slug>/src/` or `run.py`.
- Move orchestrator implementation code out of top-level `artagents/*.py` into `artagents/orchestrators/<slug>/src/` or `run.py`.
- Keep top-level `artagents/` only for shared libraries and system commands: `pipeline.py`, `doctor.py`, `setup_cli.py`, schemas, shared path/config/helpers.
- Delete `bin/` wrappers if they are not needed, or make them generated/ultra-thin entrypoints only. They should never own behavior.
- Update imports/tests/docs to the canonical locations.
- No compatibility aliases for old locations.

That gives us one mental model: **folder equals capability**. All complexity is private inside that folder.

> DEVELOPER

Let's deploy a few subagents to execute that cleanup, break it down into sensible seams, no exceptoions

> AGENT

I’ll use the ArtAgents repo skill for the cleanup rules, then split the migration by ownership so agents do not edit the same files. I’ll keep the main thread on coordination and integration rather than duplicating their work.

> AGENT

The split I’m using is three write scopes: pipeline-step executors, standalone/service executors, and orchestrators/onboarding references. Each worker owns a disjoint set of folders plus the tests/imports for that slice; the invariant is “folder owns behavior, top-level callable modules disappear except system/shared modules.”

> AGENT

I checked the tree. The executor/orchestrator cleanup is good, but the repo still has a few level problems outside that migration.

The clean model I’d use:

```text
artagents/
  orchestrators/   # workflow coordinators
  executors/       # runnable tools
  elements/        # render building blocks
  doctors/         # diagnostics / audits / repo health, if we want this concept
  utilities/       # cross-cutting helpers used by many things
  domains/         # shared domain libraries, e.g. hype/reigh/rendering
  contracts/       # schemas and interface contracts
```

What still feels misplaced:

- `reviewers/` at repo root should not be a separate top-level package. It is only used by `refine`, so it should probably move into `artagents/executors/refine/src/reviewers/`.
- Root `reigh_data.py` is now just a thin launcher. Under “no shims”, it should go. Use `pipeline.py reigh-data` or `bin/reigh_data.py`, but not both.
- `bin/` is still a launcher layer. If we are strict about no shims, `bin/` should either be removed entirely or treated as generated/dev convenience only. The real agent entrypoint should be `pipeline.py executors run ...` / `pipeline.py orchestrators run ...`.
- `.artagents/performers` and `.artagents/nodes` are stale local state names. They should be removed or migrated to `.artagents/elements`, `.artagents/executors`, etc. Even if ignored, they confuse the mental model.
- `artagents/social_publish.py` is only backing `upload_youtube`; it should move into `artagents/executors/upload_youtube/src/social_publish.py` unless we plan multiple social executors.
- `arrangement_rules.py`, `enriched_arrangement.py`, and probably `text_match.py` are not global ArtAgents concepts. They are hype/editing domain code. I’d put them under something like `artagents/domains/hype/`.
- `effects_catalog.py` is basically an element registry facade. It should likely become `artagents/elements/catalog.py`.
- `llm_clients.py` is genuinely cross-cutting, so it fits a future `artagents/utilities/llm_clients.py`.
- `audit/` is cross-cutting runtime observability. It can stay top-level, or become `artagents/doctors/audit` if we make “doctors” a first-class concept. I would keep it top-level only if we view audit as runtime infrastructure, not a diagnostic tool.

So: executors/orchestrators are fixed, but I would do one more cleanup pass around **root/package taxonomy**:

1. Move root `reviewers/` into `refine`.
2. Remove root `reigh_data.py`.
3. Decide whether `bin/` survives; if yes, document it as dev launchers, not canonical API.
4. Remove stale `.artagents/performers` / `.artagents/nodes`.
5. Create `artagents/domains/hype/` for hype-specific shared code.
6. Create `artagents/utilities/` for true cross-cutting helpers.
7. Move element catalog code into `artagents/elements/`.

That gives the top level a much clearer rule: only broad concepts and shared infrastructure live there; workflow/tool-specific code lives with its owner.

> DEVELOPER

Yeah, I think that looks good and would the domains actually be useful right now?

> AGENT

Yes, but only if we keep it narrow.

A `domains/` layer is useful right now because some code is clearly **not an executor** but also **not globally useful**:

- `arrangement_rules.py`
- `enriched_arrangement.py`
- `text_match.py`
- some refine/reviewer concepts if they are shared beyond `refine`
- maybe timeline/hype metadata helpers later

Those are “hype editing domain” concepts. Putting them under:

```text
artagents/domains/hype/
  arrangement_rules.py
  enriched_arrangement.py
  text_match.py
```

makes the package easier to read:

- `executors/` = runnable tools
- `orchestrators/` = workflows
- `elements/` = render components
- `utilities/` = truly generic helpers
- `domains/hype/` = shared concepts for hype-cut/editing workflows

But I would **not** create many domains yet. Start with only:

```text
artagents/domains/hype/
```

Do not add `domains/reigh`, `domains/social`, `domains/media`, etc. until at least two files clearly need them. Otherwise it becomes architecture cosplay.

So yes: `domains/hype` is useful now because it removes ambiguity from top-level shared files without forcing them into executor folders where they do not belong.

> DEVELOPER

why can't execturos simply use/depend on other executors?

> AGENT

They can. That is actually the cleaner model.

An executor depending on another executor is fine when the dependency is a **real callable capability** with its own inputs/outputs. Example:

```text
sprite_sheet
  depends on generate_image
```

or:

```text
thumbnail_maker orchestrator
  calls visual_understand
  calls generate_image
  calls render
```

What I would avoid is using “executor dependency” as a place to hide ordinary shared code.

Good executor dependency:
- `sprite_sheet` calls `generate_image` because image generation is its own tool.
- `publish` calls `upload_youtube` if publishing branches by platform.
- `understand` calls `audio_understand`, `visual_understand`, `video_understand`.

Bad executor dependency:
- `refine` calls an `arrangement_rules` executor just to reuse a validation function.
- `cut` calls a `text_match` executor just to tokenize text.
- `render` calls a `theme_schema` executor just to validate a JSON object.

So the rule should be:

> If it is a callable user/agent capability, make it an executor and let other executors/orchestrators depend on it. If it is pure library logic, keep it in `domains/` or `utilities/`.

This gives us composability without turning every helper function into a fake node.

> DEVELOPER

So - all in - what should we do?

> AGENT

Do one more cleanup pass, but keep it disciplined.

Canonical structure:

```text
artagents/
  executors/       # every runnable tool
  orchestrators/   # workflows that coordinate executors
  elements/        # render building blocks
  domains/hype/    # hype/editing domain logic shared by executors
  utilities/       # truly generic helpers
  contracts/       # schemas/contracts
  audit/           # runtime audit/lineage infra, keep for now
  doctor.py        # system command
  setup_cli.py     # system command
  pipeline.py      # system command / legacy workflow entry
```

Concrete changes I’d make:

1. Move `reviewers/` into `artagents/executors/refine/src/reviewers/`.
   It is refine-owned behavior, not repo-level structure.

2. Move hype-specific shared code:
   - `artagents/arrangement_rules.py` -> `artagents/domains/hype/arrangement_rules.py`
   - `artagents/enriched_arrangement.py` -> `artagents/domains/hype/enriched_arrangement.py`
   - `artagents/text_match.py` -> `artagents/domains/hype/text_match.py`

3. Move generic helpers:
   - `artagents/llm_clients.py` -> `artagents/utilities/llm_clients.py`

4. Move element facade:
   - `artagents/effects_catalog.py` -> `artagents/elements/catalog.py`

5. Move social publisher into the executor that owns it:
   - `artagents/social_publish.py` -> `artagents/executors/upload_youtube/src/social_publish.py`

6. Remove stale/confusing local leftovers:
   - `.artagents/performers`
   - `.artagents/nodes`
   - maybe root `reigh_data.py`

7. Keep `bin/` only if we explicitly label it as non-canonical dev launchers.
   Canonical agent API should be:
   - `python3 pipeline.py executors ...`
   - `python3 pipeline.py orchestrators ...`
   - `python3 pipeline.py elements ...`

8. Add structure tests so this does not regress:
   - no top-level runnable executor/orchestrator modules
   - no `performers`, `conductors`, `nodes`, `curated`, `builtin`, `actions`
   - top-level `artagents/*.py` allowlist only
   - executor folders require `executor.yaml`, `SKILL.md`, `run.py`
   - orchestrator folders require `orchestrator.yaml`, `SKILL.md`, `run.py`

I would **not** create more domains yet. Start with only `domains/hype`. Executors can depend on other executors when they need actual capabilities, and shared pure logic goes into `domains/hype` or `utilities`.

> DEVELOPER

but why are we still creating the domain if we can just import from a differnet tool? What are elements

> AGENT

Because “importing from a different tool” quietly makes that other tool two things at once:

1. A callable capability.
2. A shared library package.

That gets messy fast.

Example: if `cut` imports validation helpers from `arrange`, then `arrange` is no longer just an executor. It becomes a library that other executors depend on. Now changing `arrange/run.py` for CLI behavior can accidentally break `cut`.

Better split:

- Executors depend on other executors when they need the **result of a capability**.
- Executors import domain/utilities code when they need **shared pure logic**.

So:

```text
cut calls arrange executor
```

makes sense if `cut` needs an actual arrangement file produced.

```text
cut imports compile_arrangement_plan()
```

should come from:

```text
artagents/domains/hype/arrangement_rules.py
```

not from the `arrange` executor.

That keeps executors clean: they are tools, not accidental libraries.

Elements are different. Elements are **user-editable render building blocks**, not Python tools. They are the things that appear inside timelines/themes and Remotion rendering:

```text
artagents/elements/bundled/effects/text-card/
  meta.json
  schema.json
  defaults.json
  component.tsx
```

Examples:
- effects: `text-card`
- animations: `fade-up`, `type-on`
- transitions: `cross-fade`

They are not executors because you don’t “run” `fade-up` as a tool. You reference it in a timeline or theme, and the renderer uses it.

So the clean distinction is:

- **Executor**: callable work unit. “Generate image”, “render video”, “analyze audio”.
- **Orchestrator**: workflow that coordinates executors. “Make hype cut”, “make thumbnail”.
- **Element**: editable render component referenced by output. “Fade up this text”, “use this transition”.
- **Domain**: shared concepts/rules used by executors/orchestrators. “What is a valid hype arrangement?”

> DEVELOPER

but is it not very confusing for a dev to know when something should be a domain or just inside their code?

> AGENT

Yes, it can be confusing. So the rule should be conservative:

**Default: keep code inside the executor/orchestrator folder.**

Only promote something to `domains/hype` when all three are true:

1. It is used by multiple executors/orchestrators.
2. It represents a product/domain concept, not just plumbing.
3. It is stable enough that sharing it will reduce duplication rather than create coupling.

Examples that qualify:
- `arrangement_rules.py`: shared hype-cut rules.
- `enriched_arrangement.py`: shared representation for reviewing/refining a cut.
- maybe `text_match.py`, but only because it supports the same hype review domain.

Examples that should stay local:
- prompt formatting for `generate_image`
- YouTube payload shaping for `upload_youtube`
- sprite slicing logic for `sprite_sheet`
- thumbnail candidate planning unless another workflow also uses it
- VibeComfy internals

So the dev mental model becomes:

```text
Is it runnable?
  yes -> executor or orchestrator folder

Is it a render component?
  yes -> element folder

Is it only used by this one tool/workflow?
  yes -> keep inside that folder/src

Is it shared by multiple hype/editing tools and describes the domain?
  yes -> domains/hype

Is it generic plumbing, like paths, API clients, JSON helpers?
  yes -> utilities
```

That is not too much taxonomy because `domains/` is the exception, not the default. The folder is there only to stop shared product logic from leaking into random executor folders.

> DEVELOPER

And do you think it's better for us to, there's two ways to solve this. One is for us to use AI to actually just make it so they function really well as packages. So it's cleaning up all the time when we need to make it function as a package. Another one is to actually just, like when users start adding nodes, to actually just start to kind of update them. You know, if something is used multiple times to actually add that as a domain thing and then make it so that we send the PR to them to use that instead of using their own code, which do you think is nicer?

> AGENT

The nicer model is the second one, with automation helping but not silently rewriting people’s packages.

Do this:

**User packages stay self-contained by default.**  
When someone adds an executor/orchestrator, their folder owns its code. We should not immediately “clean it up” into shared domains, because that makes contribution feel unpredictable and can break their mental model.

Then have ArtAgents detect opportunities:

- duplicated logic across packages
- multiple packages implementing the same domain concept
- package code importing another executor’s internals
- repeated requirements/config/prompts/schema fragments
- common validation rules emerging

When that happens, ArtAgents should propose a domain extraction:

```text
Suggestion:
3 executors implement similar hype arrangement validation.
Extract shared logic to artagents/domains/hype/arrangement_rules.py.

Affected:
- executors/arrange
- executors/cut
- executors/refine

Suggested PR:
- add shared domain helper
- update those packages to import it
- keep behavior unchanged
```

For external/user-owned packages, we should generate a PR or patch suggestion, not mutate them silently.

So the workflow should be:

1. **Accept package as-is** if it follows the executor/orchestrator folder contract.
2. **Run doctor/audit** to detect duplication, coupling, and bad imports.
3. **Suggest extraction** only when reuse is proven.
4. **Create a PR/patch** that moves shared stable concepts into `domains/`.
5. **Keep local package compatibility explicit**, not hidden via shims.

This is better because it keeps contribution simple:

> “Put your tool in one folder and make it work.”

Then ArtAgents can evolve the shared architecture over time:

> “This pattern is now common enough to promote.”

So I would not build an AI janitor that constantly reorganizes packages on ingestion. I’d build an **architecture doctor** that reviews packages, proposes cleanups, and can generate PRs when the owner agrees.

> AGENT

Executed the cleanup.

What changed:
- Moved hype shared logic into `artagents/domains/hype/`:
  - `arrangement_rules.py`
  - `enriched_arrangement.py`
  - `text_match.py`
- Moved generic LLM plumbing into `artagents/utilities/llm_clients.py`.
- Moved element catalog into `artagents/elements/catalog.py`.
- Moved refine-only reviewers into `artagents/executors/refine/src/reviewers/`.
- Moved YouTube social publishing helper into `artagents/executors/upload_youtube/src/social_publish.py`.
- Removed stale/root leftovers:
  - root `reviewers/`
  - root `reigh_data.py`
  - `.artagents/performers`
  - `.artagents/nodes`
- Strengthened `artagents/structure.py` so doctor now enforces the cleaner top-level package allowlist and rejects stale local concept dirs.

Current top-level `artagents/*.py` is now only:

```text
__init__.py
_paths.py
doctor.py
pipeline.py
setup_cli.py
structure.py
theme_schema.py
timeline.py
```

Validation passed:
- `python3 pipeline.py doctor`
- `python3 pipeline.py executors validate`
- `python3 pipeline.py orchestrators validate`
- `python3 pipeline.py elements validate`
- focused cleanup suite: `112 passed, 81 subtests passed`
- old import leak check: `old import leaks: []`

I did not run the entire repo suite because there was already a known media-fixture smoke issue from the earlier pass, but the cleanup-relevant surface is covered.
